## 2026-05-03 — D-ENCRYPTION-WIRING-IMPLEMENTED

**Decision:** Execute ADR-ENCRYPTION-WIRING-01 (Adopted 2026-05-02) end-to-end. Three artefacts shipped:
1. New helper module at `/website/src/lib/encryption-helpers.ts` — `EncryptedField` interface, generic helpers (`encryptForStorage`, `decryptFromStorage`), D14b-named wrappers (`encryptDeferralPayload`, `decryptDeferralPayload`).
2. Companion Jest test file at `/website/src/lib/__tests__/encryption-helpers.test.ts` — round-trip + KG7 shape invariants + AES-GCM auth-tag tamper detection (10 test blocks).
3. Schema migration applied to production Supabase (`sagereasoning-us`): `open_deferrals` + `deferral_resolutions` tables created with 4 user-defined indexes, 2 RLS policies, ON DELETE CASCADE for R17c. SQL committed at `/operations/migrations/2026-05-03-encryption-wiring-schema.sql` for audit trail.

Adapted from ADR Action Items 4–10 per Pattern B (production-only Supabase, no separate staging) under founder-approved Path A (pre-deploy unit tests + post-deploy SQL verification). Step 1 — backup status — resolved as Option 1 (key in production + backups verified within last month; password manager + paper copies confirmed against Vercel value). ADR Action Items 1–3 (key generation + ceremony) skipped per Option 1 path. ADR Action Item 5 (integration dry-run) replaced by 25/25 standalone-validator assertions on algorithm + storage shape + `tsc --noEmit` exit 0 across the codebase. ADR Action Item 8 (route-level AC4 invocation testing) deferred to Phase-2 pass-1 commencement (route does not exist this session).

Critical Change Protocol surfaced verbatim per ADR §"Critical Change Protocol responses" with adaptations for this session's actual scope. Founder explicit approval recorded specific to named risks (rollback paths B = DROP TABLE; C = revert commit; founder-performable verification queries; AC7 not engaged). Schema verification confirmed by founder via 4 SQL queries: V1 (2 tables present), V2 (RLS enabled both), V3 (4 indexes), V4 (2 policies). Vercel deployment confirmed green post-push. Tables remain dormant under `MENTOR_RAG_V1=false` default; first writes land at Phase-2 pass-1 commencement (separate Critical session).

**Reasoning:** Phase-2 pass-1 Precondition 4 (encryption wiring) is the only remaining design-side work before Phase-2 pass 1 can commence per D21 § Precondition 4 + the streams-1-2 session-close inventory. The ADR pre-drafted the architecture (Decisions 1–5) and the Critical Change Protocol responses; this session executes the ADR's Action Items.

The Pattern B adaptation (production-only Supabase) was a session-shape decision driven by the founder's actual operational reality. The ADR's Decision 3 SQL is preserved; the staging/production split (ADR Action Items 4 + 6) collapses into a single production migration with idempotent guards (`CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` + `DROP POLICY IF EXISTS` for safe re-runs). The integration dry-run (ADR Action Item 5) is replaced by a more conservative validation strategy: pre-deploy unit tests on the helpers + post-deploy SQL verification on the schema. The full encrypt-write-read-decrypt round-trip lands at Phase-2 pass-1 commencement when the route does its first real write — a less circuitous validation path that avoids local Vercel CLI setup for a non-coder founder while preserving R17b risk mitigation via different mechanisms.

The helpers themselves are a literal pass-through to `server-encryption.ts` (already in production via `mentor-profile-store.ts`). The only new surface is the storage-shape adaptation: the `EncryptedField` interface splits the `ServerEncryptedPayload` into `ciphertext` (TEXT column) + `meta` (JSONB column) with KG7-aware shape (plain object literal, never JSON.stringify-ed).

Per founder direction at the predecessor session, the first work this session was Step 1 — verify `MENTOR_ENCRYPTION_KEY` backup status against the existing production value. Founder confirmed Option 1 (key in production + backups verified recently). The Vercel "needs attention" advisory on `MENTOR_ENCRYPTION_KEY` (and same on `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` + `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`) was diagnosed as a benign platform recommendation (suggest marking as Sensitive + optionally rotating at source) — informational only, NOT a fork-state, logged for future attention. No key was generated, rotated, or modified this session.

## 2026-05-03 — D-PHASE-2-PASS-1-SCOPE-BLOCK-AND-REPLAN

**Decision:** Pause Phase-2 pass-1 commencement at the entry gate (before any code work). Re-plan Phase-2 pass-1 as a **dependency-respecting sequence of bounded sub-sessions** rather than a single Critical session. The single-session scope assumed by the predecessor next-session prompt (`2026-05-03-post-encryption-wiring-NEXT-SESSION-PROMPT.md`) is materially wrong: Step 7 ("Implement engine integration") was sized at 30 minutes "stitching pre-existing modules"; Part A reads of the codebase confirmed no such modules exist. The alt-3 deterministic engine — Layer 1 translator (D10), 10 deterministic rules (D8), 12-position sequencer with two-pass + back-edge logic (D9), trigger-code dispatch (D13), Layer 3 NULL projection (D11 Table 4b minimum) — is fully **Designed** but not **Scaffolded** in code. The new route at `/api/mentor/private/deferral-resolve` cannot meaningfully exist without an engine to call; D14b §"Founder-performable verification protocol" Verifications 2, 3, 4, 6, 7, 8 cannot be tested without the engine producing OPEN_DEFERRAL flags upstream.

The new build order, founder-approved at this session via AskUserQuestion (Path "Pause and replan, check for previous build order, review against current status, propose new order"):

| Sub-session | Substance | Risk | Estimate |
|---|---|---|---|
| **A** | `corpus_passages` schema migration (D5) — pgvector + tsvector + RLS + 6 indexes | Standard | 1–2 h |
| **B** | Index population script + D-A16 catalogue insertion (20 stems) | Standard | 2–4 h |
| **C** | Retrieval interface (D6) + re-ranker (D7) | Standard | 4–8 h |
| **D** | Layer 1 translator (D10) — Sonnet wiring + structured-output validation | Standard | 3–5 h |
| **E1–E4** | 10 deterministic rules (D8) + 12-position sequencer (D9) + trigger dispatch (D13) | Standard per piece | 4 × 4–10 h |
| **F** | Layer 3 NULL projection (D11 Table 4b only — minimal for D14b) | Standard | 2–3 h |
| **G** | New route + page + R20a ninth-route addition (Steps 5/6/8) — pre-deploy | Standard | 4–6 h |
| **H** | env flag flip + 8 founder verifications (Steps 10/12) | **Critical** | 1–3 h |

Total realistic effort: ~35–65 hours across ~10–12 sub-sessions. Each sub-session is bounded, founder-verifiable between sessions per 0c, and respects PR1 (single-endpoint proof on each substrate piece before integration). The Critical Change Protocol applies at Sub-session H only — Sub-sessions A–G are Standard risk by themselves.

**Founder-approved next session: Sub-session A (`corpus_passages` schema migration).** Smallest, lowest-risk starting point. Standard risk per 0d-ii. Pattern matches the predecessor encryption-wiring session: pgvector + tsvector schema applied via Supabase SQL Editor; founder verifies via 4 SQL queries; no code deploy this session. The next-session prompt at `/operations/handoffs/founder/2026-05-03-corpus-passages-schema-NEXT-SESSION-PROMPT.md` carries the full opening protocol.

**Reasoning:** Three forces converged at this session:

1. **The predecessor next-session prompt was scope-blocked at Step 7.** "Engine integration — 30 minutes mostly stitching pre-existing modules" was the load-bearing claim. Searches against the codebase (`grep -rilE 'mechanism_1|mechanism_5|mechanism_10|prohairesis_filter|passion_root_detection|virtue_domain_engaged|ELEMENT_FUSION|EUPATHEIA_BOUNDARY|REFLECTION_NARRATIVE_THIN|layer_1_output|layer_3' lib/ app/`) returned zero matches in active code paths. The only file referencing these terms in `/website/src/` is the D14b-encryption helpers JSDoc (introductory commentary; no implementation). The existing `sage-reason-engine.ts` (612 lines) implements the **V3 LLM-end-to-end engine** — not the alt-3 deterministic engine.

2. **D21 sequenced steps without separating engine substrate from route build.** D21's "Phase-2 Pass 1 build steps" lists 11 steps (Schema → Encryption → Index population → D-A16 catalogue → Route → Page → Engine integration → R20a → Verifier → env flag → CCP). The implicit assumption is that "Engine integration" is a stitching step. It is not — it is the bulk of the work, distributed across Layer 1 + 10 rules + sequencer + trigger dispatch + Layer 3 minimum. D21's sequencing is structurally correct (the ordering of pieces) but operationally wrong (the time + risk allocation per piece).

3. **Founder honoured PR1 + the founder's stated working pace.** Per the founder's project preferences ("I work in fast, bounded phases"; "I verify your work between sessions, not in real time"; "Risk and Side Effects — explain the specific risk in plain language and get my approval"), pushing through Step 7 with stub-implementations or rushed engine code would violate both the working pace and the verification rhythm. The founder's selection at the AskUserQuestion gate ("Pause and replan, check for previous build order, review against current status, propose new order") confirms the principled path. PR1 (single-endpoint proof before surface rollout) is preserved at a deeper level: each substrate piece (Sub-sessions A–F) reaches Verified status before the integration session (G) attempts the route + page; Sub-session G itself reaches Verified before the Critical activation session (H) flips the env flag.

**Honest disclosure:** the scope-block could have been surfaced earlier. The encryption-wiring ADR-Adopted session (2026-05-02) and the encryption-wiring implementation session (2026-05-03) both treated Phase-2 pass-1 commencement as the next-next-session candidate without auditing whether the engine substrate existed. Neither session was scope-broken at its own scope (both delivered what they promised — the ADR + the helpers + the schema + Vercel green) but the cumulative narrative implied pass-1 was a single session away. The corrective action is this entry + the new sub-session sequence; the lesson is that "Designed" status (per 0a) is not "Scaffolded" status, and a build prompt that names a build step assumes the substrate is at least Scaffolded.

## 2026-05-03 — D-PHASE-2-PASS-1-REPLAN-EFFICIENCY-REFINEMENT

**Decision:** Refine the Phase-2 pass-1 build sequence (per `D-PHASE-2-PASS-1-SCOPE-BLOCK-AND-REPLAN-2026-05-03`) with three efficiency moves, founder-approved this session via AskUserQuestion. The moves: (1) **Standing Protocol Cache** — new document at `/adopted/standing-protocol-cache.md` replaces full re-reads of manifest + session-opening-protocol + knowledge-gaps register at every session open with a one-stop reference. Full re-reads only when work specifically requires. (2) **Lean Standard-risk templates** — for Sub-sessions A–G, the decision-log entry shrinks to ~20-30 lines, the session close shrinks to ~40-60 lines, the next-session prompt shrinks to ~70-120 lines. Sub-session H (Critical) keeps the full templates per the existing protocol. (3) **Sub-session A + B combined** — schema migration + index population + D-A16 catalogue insertion in a single ~3-4 hour session. Founder rejected (4) pattern-precedent shorthand at the same gate; the lean templates already absorb most of (4)'s benefits.

The amended Phase-2 pass-1 sub-session sequence:

| Sub-session | Substance | Risk | Estimate |
|---|---|---|---|
| **A+B (combined)** | `corpus_passages` schema (D5) + index population (stoic-brain.ts → corpus_passages) + D-A16 catalogue insertion (20 stems) | Standard | 3–4 h |
| **C** | Retrieval interface (D6) + re-ranker (D7) | Standard | 4–8 h |
| **D** | Layer 1 translator (D10) — Sonnet wiring + structured-output validation | Standard | 3–5 h |
| **E1–E4** | 10 deterministic rules (D8) + 12-position sequencer (D9) + trigger dispatch (D13) | Standard per piece | 4 × 4–10 h |
| **F** | Layer 3 NULL projection (D11 Table 4b only) | Standard | 2–3 h |
| **G** | New route + page + R20a ninth-route addition | Standard | 4–6 h |
| **H** | env flag flip + 8 founder verifications | **Critical** | 1–3 h |

Total realistic effort post-refinement: ~30–55 hours across ~9–11 sub-sessions (down from 35–65 hours / 10–12 sub-sessions per the prior re-plan). The savings come from collapsing A+B + the lean-templates overhead reduction (~60–90 min saved per Sub-session C–G). Sub-session H's Critical full-form discipline is preserved.

**Reasoning:** The prior re-plan (`D-PHASE-2-PASS-1-SCOPE-BLOCK-AND-REPLAN`) named the dependency-respecting sequence but did not address the per-session overhead. Founder calculated that protocol re-reads + full-form decision-log entries + full-form closes + full-form next-session prompts consume ~1.5–2.5 hours of overhead per sub-session against a working budget of 1–4 hours. For Sub-sessions A and B specifically, overhead would exceed substantive work. The three approved moves recover that overhead without compromising the audit trail or the Critical-risk discipline:

- **Move 1 (Standing Protocol Cache).** Per the session-opening protocol's own design (Element 2 — "Read the tier's sources in the order listed"), the canonical-sources file already abstracts the read sequence. The cache extends the same logic: it abstracts the standing answers (manifest rules per category; model selection table; signals; risk classification defaults; lean templates). Sessions read the cache (~3 min) instead of the manifest + protocol + knowledge-gaps in full (~25–40 min). When governance changes, the cache updates in the same session as the governance change (Standard risk amendment; cache-drift entries logged via `D-CACHE-DRIFT-RESOLVED-…`).

- **Move 2 (Lean Standard-risk templates).** The full templates were designed for Critical-risk sessions where every section has audit value. For Standard-risk schema migrations, registry updates, and decision-log governance work, several sections (Orchestration Reminder; expanded Risk Classification Record; "Where We Are in P0" extended block; exhaustive cross-reference list) have low marginal audit value because the corresponding work surface is itself lean. The lean templates preserve the load-bearing fields (Verification Step, Files Touched, Risk Classification, Status, Founder Verification with commit commands) and drop the boilerplate. The Critical full form remains for Sub-session H (env flag flip — the load-bearing activation step) and for any future Critical work.

- **Move 3 (Combine A + B).** Schema + index population + D-A16 insertion is structurally one piece of work — schema declares columns; population fills them. Splitting into two sessions adds an entire close + prompt + open cycle for no architectural benefit. The combined session reaches Verified status via 4 SQL queries (schema verification, same as predecessor encryption-wiring shape) plus a corpus_passages row count + spot-check on D-A16 stem rows.

The trade-offs are honestly named:

- **Cache drift risk.** Mitigation: the cache document includes its own update discipline section (in-session amendment when governance changes; `D-CACHE-DRIFT-RESOLVED-…` entries for tracking). The cache header also explicitly states "the governance documents are authoritative; the cache is a reference convenience, not a substitute."
- **Trail thinning at lean-form entries.** Mitigation: lean form preserves the load-bearing audit fields. The boilerplate that's dropped (Orchestration Reminder, expanded Risk Classification Record, etc.) is itself derivable from the cache's standing answers — re-deriving from the cache at audit time produces the same content the boilerplate carried.
- **Combined-session risk for A + B.** The combined session is larger (~3–4 hours) than either piece alone. If founder needs to pause mid-session, the schema apply + verification can land in one bounded segment, and the index population can resume at next session.

- `/operations/handoffs/founder/2026-05-03-phase-2-pass-1-scope-block-and-replan-close.md` — updated in place with the refinement (additional artefact list, amended next-session pointer, updated commit instructions).
- `/operations/handoffs/founder/2026-05-03-corpus-passages-schema-NEXT-SESSION-PROMPT.md` — replaced with the lean A+B combined form (~120 lines instead of ~400+).

The cache document can be moved to `/archive/` and the original full templates restored to use. The Sub-session A+B combination can be re-split if the combined session proves unwieldy in practice.

## 2026-05-03 — D-CORPUS-PASSAGES-SCHEMA

**Decision:** Apply the `corpus_passages` schema to production Supabase (`sagereasoning-us`) per D5 §"The corpus_passages table — schema". Schema is Live; population is paused at script-ready state and resumes at next session per local-Node blocker (founder Mac shell does not have Node available; founder does not want to use Terminal). Sub-session A+B combined splits in practice into A (this session — schema only) + B (next session — population). Two consequential session-shape decisions logged:

1. **Embedding deferral to Sub-session C.** OpenAI `text-embedding-3-small` requires `OPENAI_API_KEY` which is not in `.env.local` and the founder has no OpenAI account today. All `embedding` values land as NULL during the population pass; tsvector_en (BM25) auto-populates via the schema's trigger (no external API). Sub-session C absorbs OpenAI key setup + embedding generation pass alongside D6 retrieval interface + D7 re-ranker. The ivfflat embedding index is correspondingly commented out in the schema migration (would be useless on a NULL column; pgvector centroid computation needs data). Re-introduce at Sub-session C. **Revisit condition:** Sub-session C commencement.
2. **Catalogue insertion scope.** Founder selected "All 27 stems" (not 20 per outdated count in prompt; not 2-only minimum per D14b). Path B for ritual stems (synthetic `intake_tier: 1`) per the D-A16 catalogue's documentation default — avoids D5 schema amendment (Elevated risk). **Revisit condition:** if D6 retrieval surfaces issues with the synthetic intake_tier dispatch.

The Node-on-PATH blocker emerged at Step 6; founder selected Path C (stabilise + close) per stated working pace ("fast, bounded phases"; "stabilise to a known-good state and close"). Population script is written + parsing-validated; resumes at next session.

## 2026-05-03 — D-CORPUS-PASSAGES-POPULATION

**Decision:** Both `corpus_passages` population scripts (corpus rows from `stoic-brain-compiled.ts` + 27 D-A16 catalogue stems) executed against `supabase-us` Supabase project via founder's local Node 24.15.0; final `corpus_passages` count = **186 rows** (159 corpus + 27 D-A16 stems); `embedding` column NULL across all rows per Sub-session C deferral; KG7 shape verified at retrieval time (slot_fields stored as JSONB arrays, not stringified).

**Reasoning:** Resumes Sub-session B from `D-CORPUS-PASSAGES-SCHEMA-2026-05-03`'s paused-at-script-ready state. Founder selected Path 1 (local Node install) via session-open AskUserQuestion after a follow-up AskUserQuestion explained what Node install entails. Sandbox-execution path was attempted first to save the founder install time but failed at the Supabase fetch boundary (HTTP 403 from sandbox outbound proxy on the `*.supabase.co` host) — sandbox network restriction is fixed and cannot be relaxed at session time. Founder then installed Node 24.15.0 (current LTS) and ran both scripts via three Terminal commands (`node --version` to verify; corpus population script; D-A16 stems script). Both scripts completed with `Errors 0`.

**Files touched:**
- `/operations/migrations/2026-05-03-d-a16-catalogue-population.mjs` — new file (~480 lines); 27 D-A16 catalogue stems as `focus_question_stem` rows; KG7-aware (`canonical_mechanism` + `slot_fields` as plain JS arrays/objects); Path B for ritual stems (synthetic `intake_tier: 1`; `trigger_condition` carries dispatch); per-row schema-validation pre-flight checks against the four corpus_passages CHECK constraints.
- `/operations/decision-log.md` — this entry appended (~80 lines).

- `/operations/handoffs/founder/2026-05-XX-retrieval-and-rerank-NEXT-SESSION-PROMPT.md` — new Sub-session C next-session prompt (~120 lines).
- `/node_modules/@supabase` — symlink → `../website/node_modules/@supabase`. Created during sandbox-path probing (`ln -s` from sandbox bash). Necessary at script-run time: without it, Node module resolution fails for the bare `@supabase/supabase-js` import in any script living outside `/website/`. Documented as load-bearing for future migration scripts. `node_modules/` is `.gitignore`d so the symlink is not committed.

**Risk classification:** Standard under 0d-ii. Pure data-write population; idempotent (upsert by `passage_id` UNIQUE); no application code touched; no auth/encryption/safety surface; no production-affecting deploy. AC7 not engaged. PR6 not engaged. Critical Change Protocol not engaged.

**Rollback path:** `TRUNCATE corpus_passages;` in Supabase SQL Editor returns the table to empty. Then re-run the two scripts to repopulate. The schema (D5 — `D-CORPUS-PASSAGES-SCHEMA-2026-05-03`) and its indexes are untouched.

**Verification step (founder-performable, executed):**

```sql

-- Expected: 186 ✓

  WHERE passage_type = 'focus_question_stem' GROUP BY jsonb_typeof(slot_fields);

-- P4: Pass-1 critical stems present

  WHERE trigger_condition IN ('EUPATHEIA_BOUNDARY', 'PRAXIS_MOTIVATION_AMBIGUITY')
  GROUP BY trigger_condition;
```

Founder confirmed: "all pass".

## 2026-05-04 — D-RETRIEVAL-RERANK-IMPLEMENTED

**Decision:** D6 retrieval interface (`retrievePassages`) and D7 re-ranker (`reRank`, heuristic policy) implemented as TypeScript modules under `/website/src/lib/rag/` with co-located types; two Postgres RPC functions (`match_passages_bm25`, `match_passages_vector`) created in production Supabase to wrap the pgvector + ts_rank_cd queries D6's pipeline calls; verification harness at `/website/scripts/test-retrieval-rerank.ts` run end-to-end with 5/5 expected matches in top-3 plus working per-request cache (warm=0ms). D6 + D7 reach **Verified** status. Two Phase-2 production observation candidates surfaced: BM25 channel returned 0 results across all 5 queries (websearch_to_tsquery default-AND across 4-term queries does not match short corpus chunks); first-query latencies exceeded D6 targets (3774ms cold for Q1 vs 500ms target) — vector-only path produced correct ordering regardless.

**Reasoning:** Sub-session C-bis per `/operations/handoffs/founder/2026-05-XX-sub-session-C-bis-NEXT-SESSION-PROMPT.md`. Founder confirmed Path A (rag/ subdirectory) and types-alongside layout at session open. The session prompt's procedure did not anticipate that pgvector + ts_rank_cd queries cannot be expressed through Supabase JS's standard query builder — surfaced as a gap, founder approved adding the two RPC functions as a Standard-risk schema migration step (the canonical Supabase + pgvector pattern). Test harness written + run successfully; founder posted full output for documentation despite the prior "stop" signal — interpreted as "complete this session's work cleanly with the data we now have." All 5 representative query shapes returned the expected passage_id in top-3; the architecture is structurally sound.

| Q2 dichotomy of control | 0 | 40 | 40 | 986 | ✓ `stoic-brain:foundations:dichotomy_of_control:up_to_us:list` |
| Q3 oikeiosis stage | 0 | 5 | 5 | 1371 | ✓ `action:oikeiosis:stage_2_family` |
| Q4 TEMPORAL_AMBIGUITY focus stem | 0 | 1 | 1 | 748 | ✓ `tier_1:temporal_ambiguity:001` |
| Q5 passion root detection | 0 | 24 | 24 | 3229 | ✓ `passions:epithumia:definition` |
| Q1 cold (cache test) | (vector only) | (vector only) | — | 790 | (cache_hit=false) |
| Q1 warm (cache test) | — | — | — | 0 | (cache_hit=true) ✓ |

5/5 expected matches in top-3. Cost: $0.0000014 USD (estimated 72 tokens at $0.02/1M). Per-request cache works (warm=0ms).

**Open questions / findings from the run:**

1. **BM25 channel returned 0 results across all 5 queries.** `websearch_to_tsquery` defaults to AND across query terms. Each test query is 4–6 terms (e.g., "oikeiosis circle of concern self-other expansion"); short corpus chunks rarely contain ALL terms. The vector channel produces correct top-3 regardless. **Revisit condition:** Phase-2 production observation. Possible refinements: switch to `plainto_tsquery` (also AND but more lenient parsing); rewrite queries to OR-shape via `websearch_to_tsquery` syntax (`"philodoxia" OR "false judgement"`); or accept BM25 as exact-term-match and design rule queries with single-term BM25 keys. Logged for the cost-model and retrieval-observability scope per D6 §"Open questions" items 2 + 3.

2. **Latencies above D6 targets.** Cold path target 500ms; observed 748–3774ms. Q1 first-call (3774ms) likely includes OpenAI cold-start handshake. Q5 (3229ms) is also slow. Q1 cold (after warm-up earlier in the run) was 790ms — closer but still above 500ms. Vector-only retrieval functioning; the latency overhead is in OpenAI embedding (network + model invocation) plus pgvector ivfflat probe at lists=100. **Revisit condition:** Phase-2 production observation per D6 §"Retrieval target latency". Optimisation candidates: precomputed query embeddings for common rule patterns (server-side cache, not per-request); ivfflat `lists` retuning; HTTP keep-alive on the OpenAI client; or accept the latency and budget around it (Layer 1 + Layer 3 LLM calls dominate the per-request total anyway).

3. **D7 spec text vs worked-example multiplier discrepancy.** D7 §"Heuristic re-rank scoring formula" prose says "1.3 if passion matches; 1.1 if sub_passion matches"; the worked example (Rule 5 Pass-1) is consistent with the inverse — × 1.3 when sub_passion_filter matches; × 1.1 when only passion_filter is provided AND matches. Implementation follows the worked example. The verification run's results (5/5 expected matches) suggest the worked-example interpretation produces correct ordering on these queries. **Revisit condition:** Phase-2 production observation if specific mechanisms surface ranking issues per D7 §"Open questions" item 1.

4. **`degraded_retrieval` flag does not flip when BM25 returns 0 results.** Per the implementation, `degraded_retrieval` is set only when a channel throws (caught exception); zero-results-without-error is not currently treated as degradation. The Q1–Q5 runs all show `degraded_retrieval: 0/5` despite BM25=0/5. Honest disclosure: the flag is conservative (false negatives on partial-channel weakness). **Revisit condition:** Phase-2 production observation may revise the semantics if zero-result channels prove operationally meaningful. Logged.

5. **Test harness `.mts` stub.** First version was `.mts`; bash sandbox permissions prevented deletion when superseded by the `.ts` form. Founder may delete via Finder; harmless if left. **Revisit condition:** none unless the stub causes confusion in a later session.

6. **Sandbox network access.** The bash sandbox does not reach OpenAI or the project's Supabase host (verified via `curl` probe — HTTP 000). Not a project issue; a session-environment limitation. **Revisit condition:** if a future session needs sandbox-side runtime verification of network calls, plan around this constraint at session open. (The founder running the test from the Mac terminal is the workaround that worked this session.)

**Decision:** D6 + D7 wired into a new dedicated `/api/internal/retrieve` route as Sub-session D's PR1 single-endpoint proof; D6 `retrievePassages` contract extended with optional `bm25_query` parameter (additive; backward-compatible) so consumers can reformulate the BM25 channel query without affecting the vector channel embedding; ADR-001 (D6/D7 consumer wiring pattern) drafted and Adopted; verification harness `/website/scripts/verify-internal-retrieve.ts` executed with **25/25 ALL CHECKS PASSED**, exercising helpers (toBm25OrShape, validateRequest), real-Supabase + real-OpenAI wiring (5 C-bis-aligned queries → expected passage_id in top-3 for all 5; per-request cache warm path = 0ms), and BM25 reformulation lift (5/5 non-negative). Route + helpers + D6 contract reach **Verified** (script-based wiring proof); HTTP-surface verification (auth/rate-limit/JSON parsing) deferred to optional post-deploy curl.

**Reasoning:** Sub-session D per `/operations/handoffs/founder/2026-05-XX-sub-session-D-NEXT-SESSION-PROMPT.md`. Founder selected Candidate C (new dedicated internal route) at session open over Candidate A (`/api/reason` quick-depth) and Candidate B (V3 mentor reflection) for cleanest PR1 proof — no production users at risk; standard risk; rollback trivial. Founder selected "reformulate queries (BM25) + accept per-call cost" for the C-bis findings. Founder selected "fix it now" over "log + proceed", promoting the ADR's stated Phase-2 candidate ("D6 accepts a separate bm25_query") to in-session implementation. Final run: 25/25 PASS, with Q3 vector_count=5 + top-3 matching C-bis exactly.

4. **ADR text accuracy.** ADR-001 §"What this ADR does not decide" + §"Query construction discipline" both name the D6 `bm25_query` separation as a "Phase-2 / Sub-session E refinement candidate." The separation was implemented in this session. ## 2026-05-04 — D-DECISION-LOG-ARCHIVE-POLICY-ADOPTED

**Decision:** Adopted a quarterly archive policy for `/operations/decision-log.md` with monthly-granularity archive files (Option B). One-time cut performed today: entries dated before 2026-05-01 moved to two new archive files; active log reduced from 3863 lines / 622 KB to 1426 lines / 277 KB while preserving all 159 dated entries verbatim.

**Reasoning:** The Read-tool 256 KB single-call ceiling was being routed around with grep + offset reads for the active log; reasoning over the file at session-open also burned context. The cut at the 2026-04 / 2026-05 calendar boundary preserves the May design batch (Sub-sessions A–D of Phase-2 Pass-1) intact in the active log while archiving everything earlier. Quarterly cadence was chosen over monthly (modest file count; aligns with the project's existing quarterly review patterns) and over threshold-based (predictability over reactivity). Option B (monthly archive files) was chosen over Option A (single archive file) so future quarterly cuts can be sliced into per-month archives without re-reorganising existing archives. Founder noted the prompt's forecast (active log ~150–200 lines after split) was based on an assumption that the May section would be small; the May section is actually ~1400 lines because of the alt-3 design batch. The cut still buys ~63% line reduction and clears the immediate Read-tool friction; the recurring quarterly cadence addresses the longer-horizon growth.

## 2026-05-04 — D-REASON-RAG-WIRED

**Decision:** D6 + D7 RAG retrieval wired into `/api/reason` quick-depth Layer 1 via Pattern A2 (structured passages to engine, additive engine signature change). PR1 rollout phase begins — Candidate A consumer is the second consumer wired (Sub-session E1; Candidate C internal route was the first). Verification harness ran 27/27 ALL CHECKS PASSED across helpers (5), real-Supabase wiring on 3 representative `/api/reason` quick-depth fixtures (10 + 1 cache replay), and Layer 1 comparison axis (6).

**Reasoning:** Sub-session E1 per `/operations/handoffs/founder/2026-05-XX-sub-session-E1-NEXT-SESSION-PROMPT.md`. Founder selected Candidate A (`/api/reason` quick-depth) at session open per AI recommendation — Elevated risk, cleanest first rollout, Layer 1 replacement scope well-documented in route header. Founder selected Pattern A2 (structured passages to engine) over the AI-recommended Pattern A1 (passages-as-string). One concern stated once: A2 changes the engine's external contract used by 23 other callers. Resolved by making the engine signature change *additive* — `retrievedPassages?: RetrievedPassage[]` is optional; the existing `stoicBrainContext` string parameter is preserved and wins when both are provided; no existing caller is affected. Founder approved the implementation plan (retrieval shape, engine join format, Three-phase verification harness) before code was written. Verification surfaced one substantive observation (character-count gap: ~3978 chars OLD path vs 797–1128 chars NEW path per fixture) which was surfaced before declaring Verified; founder confirmed "verified — proceed" with the gap accepted as a Phase-2 production observation candidate.

Added `retrievedPassages?: RetrievedPassage[]` to `ReasonInput` interface (additive, backward-compatible). Added exported `formatRetrievedPassagesAsBlock(passages)` helper that renders passages as `STOIC BRAIN — RETRIEVED PASSAGES` block with `[<source_citation>] <text>` items joined by `\n\n---\n\n` (R7 fidelity preserved). Modified `stoicBrainBlock` derivation: prefers `stoicBrainContext` string when provided (existing behaviour); builds block from `retrievedPassages` only when string is empty/omitted. **Open questions / findings from the run:**

1. **Character-count gap between OLD and NEW Layer 1 paths.** OLD `getStoicBrainContext('quick')` returns ~3978 chars (~995 tokens) of static taxonomy. NEW retrieval-driven path returns 797–1128 chars (~200–280 tokens) of query-relevant passages — 3-5× shorter on a character basis. The structural verification passed; the substantive question — *does the engine reason as well with less philosophical reference material that's more relevant* — is genuinely open. **Revisit condition:** Phase-2 production observation. Mitigation candidates if quality regresses: bump `top_k_after_rerank` for quick depth (8 → 15-20); broaden the `passage_type_filter` to include `canonical_line` examples; or revert to Pattern A1 + concatenate the static loader's content alongside retrieved passages. Founder confirmed "verified — proceed" with the gap accepted as a known cost.

2. **F2 passion-fixture top-1 was `passions:phobos:definition`, not orge.** The input "I am furious with my colleague... I want to confront them publicly tomorrow morning" maps lexically to anger (orge under epithumia) but the retrieval surfaced phobos (fear) first. Likely cause: "tomorrow morning" reads as future-orientation, and phobos definitions are dense around future-apparent-evil framing. Not a regression — phobos + lupe + control_filter are all reasonable mechanisms for the input — but a Phase-2 observation candidate. **Revisit condition:** if Phase-2 production traffic shows passion mis-classification at the engine level downstream of Layer 1, consider adding sub-passion-specific filters or augmenting the query with passion-taxonomy terms.

3. **F3 oikeiosis-fixture top-2 to top-5 were dispreferred indifferents** (loneliness, exile, low_birth) and `terpsis`, not more `oikeiosis_*` passages. Top-1 was correct (`action:oikeiosis:stage_2_family`). Likely cause: dispreferred indifferents in the corpus are tagged with `oikeiosis_obligation` because Cicero's deliberation framework reasons about them as honourable-vs-advantageous. Tag overlap is structurally valid but the ranking shape is curious. **Revisit condition:** Phase-2 production observation; if engine output for family-oriented inputs surfaces unexpected dispreferred-indifferent reasoning, consider tightening `mechanism_filter` per fixture-shape OR introducing a `passage_type_filter` refinement specific to oikeiosis-relevant inputs.

4. **Latency at upper end of D6 budget.** F1 + F2 cold = 3265–3979ms vs D6's 500ms warm-path target. F3 = 1129ms (warmer; cache + index fully primed). Consistent with Sub-session D's 700–3700ms observation. Accepted per ADR-001 §"Risks named". **Revisit condition:** Phase-2 production observation per D6 §"Open questions" item 2 (cross-request embedding cache); if user-facing latency budget impacted, consider activating a server-side embedding cache.

5. **HTTP-layer verification deferred (same as Sub-session D).** Script-based verification proves the wiring (D6 + D7 + cache + reformulation + fallback path) but does not exercise the route's auth / rate-limit / JSON parsing / HTTP-status mapping under real HTTP traffic. The route's auth + rate-limit code paths are unchanged from the prior wiring; not a regression risk. **Revisit condition:** if Sub-session E2 needs HTTP-layer verification specifically (e.g., comparison test against production traffic), build an HTTP-call verification script then.

**Decision:** Sub-session E7. `/api/score-decision` wired via Pattern A1 + α loop pattern (single retrieve, one block reused across the option-loop) per ADR-002. The route's single LLM-bound call site (within an N-option `for` loop) now receives Layer 1 from the indexed corpus instead of compiled-string constants. The wrapper `loadLayer1BlockWithFallback` is invoked once per request with `decision.trim()` as input at standard depth, folded into the route's existing parallel-context `Promise.all` alongside L2 + L3. The returned block string is reused across every iteration of the option-scoring loop. Sibling wrapper `loadLayer1BlockWithFallback` reaches **Verified-in-place** for its third user-facing consumer on the harness pass. ADR-002 reaches **Adopted** under 0f decision-status vocabulary at session-open Step 3.

**Open questions:**

1. **`decision`-shaped input vs. corpus indexing.** A multi-option decision often phrased as a question ("Should I take the consulting offer?") may retrieve more diffusely than the prose-rich F1/F2/F3 fixtures used in the harness. Topic-shape risk family — same as the GENERATION call site of `/api/score-scenario` (E6 open question #2). The harness contract test is sufficient for proving the wrapper works on known-good inputs; production observability tracks fallback rate per route via the `/api/score-decision` routeName tag in `console.warn`. **Revisit condition:** Phase-2 production observation; if the fallback rate is high, three options open: switch to Direction β (per-option retrieve) or γ (single-retrieve, per-option rerank), revise the wrapper input shape, or accept the fallback as the dominant path (which preserves pre-E7 behaviour anyway).

2. **Per-option L1 tailoring trade-off.** Direction α's L1 cannot favour passages relevant to a specific option. The same L1 grounds every option's evaluation. Accepted at E7 per the trade-off discussion. **Revisit condition:** if Phase-2 production data shows specific options retrieve poorly (because the L1 is grounded in the decision, not the option), the open question is a depth or pattern revisit per ADR-002.

3. **Cache underutilisation under α.** The `ragCache` is declared per-request (KG1 rule 4) but the wrapper is called only once with α. Cache is unused in α's request lifecycle. Documented in ADR-002 as a known cost; not a defect. **Revisit condition:** if a future loop-pattern consumer adopts β or γ (which exercise the cache), the per-request cache convention is already in place.

4. **Pattern A1's fallback runtime path is not exercised by the harness.** Continuity from G2 (E5) + H2 (E6). I2 asserts the wrapper's success-path contract (parity with `formatRetrievedPassagesAsBlock(top)`); the catch branch + the empty-result guard fire only on actual D6/D7 failure. **Revisit condition:** if Phase-2 production observation surfaces a fallback-path issue, add a fault-injection test then.

5. **`/api/reason/helpers.ts` shim is removable but retained.** Continuity item from E3 + E4 + E5 + E6. After E7, all seven PR1-rollout user-facing consumers (4 Pattern A2 + 3 Pattern A1) are wired and none import from the shim. **Revisit condition:** a future Standard-risk session can remove the shim (eligible now).

6. **`/api/score-social` route metadata inconsistency** (open question #2 from `D-SCORE-SOCIAL-RAG-WIRED-2026-05-04`) carried forward through E5 + E6 + E7. **Revisit condition:** future Standard-risk session; eligible now.

7. **`/api/score-scenario` SCORING depth-mismatch** (open question #1 from `D-SCENARIO-RAG-WIRED-2026-05-04`) carried forward through E7. SCORING uses `MODEL_DEEP` but Layer 1 corpus tier is `'quick'`. **Revisit condition:** future Standard- or Elevated-risk session against Phase-2 production observation of scoring quality.

8. **HTTP-layer verification deferred (continuity from D + E1–E6).** Script-based verification proves the wiring; HTTP-surface auth + rate-limit + JSON parsing + R20a distress-check on the route are unchanged. **Revisit condition:** if a future session needs HTTP-layer verification specifically.

*Findings #5, #6, #7, #8 are continuity items; #1, #2, #3, #4 are session-specific (#4 is also continuity). None session-blocking.*

## 2026-05-04 — D-E8-CLEANUP-AND-DEPTH-FRAMING-2026-05-04

**Decision:** Sub-session E8. Two cleanup items executed + one architectural framing captured as the session's primary insight.

(1) `/api/reason/helpers.ts` deprecated re-export shim **deleted**. Zero importers verified by grep across `/website/src/` at session open (the shim's only matches are descriptive comments in `/website/src/lib/rag/helpers.ts:6`, `/website/src/app/api/score/route.ts:67`, and `/website/scripts/verify-reason-rag.ts:96`; the build cache `tsconfig.tsbuildinfo` regenerates on next build). Continuity item from E3 onwards now closed.

(2) `/api/score-social` response-envelope metadata corrected. Line 218 changed from `ai_model: MODEL_FAST` to `ai_model: MODEL_DEEP`; the import on line 6 changed from `import { MODEL_FAST }` to `import { MODEL_DEEP }`. The route invokes `runSageReason` at `depth: 'standard'`, which maps to `MODEL_DEEP` (Sonnet) per `sage-reason-engine.ts:366` — the previous metadata reported Haiku (`claude-haiku-4-5-20251001`) while Sonnet (`claude-sonnet-4-6`) was actually invoked. The fix makes the AI transparency metadata truthful for what runs today. Continuity item from E4 (open question #2 of D-SCORE-SOCIAL-RAG-WIRED-2026-05-04) now resolved as **Option A — metadata honesty fix**. Option B (change depth from `'standard'` to `'quick'` to use Haiku) was specifically not chosen this session — it pre-empts the architectural question (3) and changes user-facing model behaviour.

(3) **Architectural framing captured as session insight (not yet codified as a manifest constraint):** depth tiers are migration scaffolding for the translation-sandwich architecture (text → schema [Layer 1 translation, LLM] → deterministic mechanism application [no LLM] → schema → text [Layer 3 translation, LLM]). The reasoning middle is meant to be deterministic; once all consumers operate under the new system, depth-as-LLM-reasoning-tier has no job and can be retired. Depth tiers persist today only as scaffolding for the period when not all consumers have moved to the new architecture. This framing is the founder's stated design intent and reframes Candidates 3, 4, 5 from the E8 menu through the migration lens. **Status:** Adopted as the session's framing decision; explicitly NOT yet promoted to an architectural constraint (no AC8 added) or ADR. Promotion to a binding rule requires its own session with the manifest in front and a migration plan sketched.

**Reasoning:** Sub-session E8 opened with a candidate menu of six items; founder bounded scope to Candidates 1 + 2 only (deferred 3 + 4 + 5 + 6). During Candidate 2's analysis, a deeper inconsistency surfaced: `model-config.ts:5` lists `score-social` under "Haiku (fast)" while the route invokes `depth: 'standard'` → Sonnet. The two design statements disagreed. Surfacing this as a bundled-decision question (mechanism set vs. model selection) triggered the founder's clarification of the underlying architecture: depth is not a primitive; it is migration scaffolding for a translation-sandwich design where the reasoning middle is deterministic. With that framing, Option A (metadata honesty fix) is the correct reading — it makes today's state truthful while migration is incomplete; Option B (change depth) would pre-empt the migration's architectural decisions. Option A is also a single-line metadata edit on the response envelope; no model invocation changes; no user-facing reasoning-quality change; AC-12 narrowness preserved (no new LLM call introduced). `tsc --noEmit -p .` clean after both edits (exit 0).

**Files touched:**
- `/website/src/app/api/reason/helpers.ts` — **deleted**. Was a deprecated re-export shim added at E3 (D-CONSUMER-WIRING-LIFT-2026-05-04) for defensive cushioning during the Pattern S2 lift to `/lib/rag/helpers.ts`. Zero importers verified at session open.
- `/website/src/app/api/score-social/route.ts` — modified. Line 6 import: `MODEL_FAST` → `MODEL_DEEP`. Line 218 response envelope: `ai_model: MODEL_FAST` → `ai_model: MODEL_DEEP`. No other changes; route's depth choice (`'standard'`), R20a distress check (line 115), Layer 1 wiring (E4's Pattern A2 via `loadLayer1WithFallback`), and all other behaviour are unchanged.
- `/operations/decision-log.md` — this entry appended.
- `/operations/handoffs/founder/2026-05-04-sub-session-E8-close.md` — new lean session close.
- `/operations/handoffs/founder/2026-05-04-E8-NEXT-SESSION-PROMPT.md` — new next-session prompt for E9 surfacing the depth-architecture migration as the headline question.

**Risk classification:** Standard under 0d-ii. Highest-risk action this session = single-line response-envelope metadata change on `/api/score-social` (no model invocation change; no reasoning behaviour change; the change is what the response *reports*, not what the model *does*). Shim removal is module deletion with zero importers verified. AC7 NOT engaged. PR6 NOT engaged. Critical Change Protocol NOT engaged.

**Rollback path:** `git revert` of the cleanup commit restores both the shim file and the original `MODEL_FAST` metadata. The architectural framing in section (3) is documentation-only at this stage; reverting the commit does not "undo" the framing — it remains the founder's stated design intent regardless of whether the cleanup commit lands. If the framing itself is reconsidered, that's a separate decision-log entry, not a code revert.

**Open questions:**

1. **Depth-architecture migration — when does it begin?** The framing in section (3) of this decision is captured but not yet codified. Open: whether the migration becomes its own ADR (e.g., AC8 + a migration plan ADR), what the consumer-by-consumer migration sequence looks like, and what triggers the retirement of depth tiers. **Revisit condition:** E9 or a future dedicated architectural session with the manifest in front. Not session-blocking; consumers continue to function as today.

2. **Candidate 3 (`/api/score-scenario` SCORING depth-mismatch) under the migration lens.** At the E8 open, founder stated a directional preference of "change to 'deep'" if Candidate 3 is executed. Under the new framing, this becomes a holding-pattern fix (makes today's state internally consistent) rather than an architectural decision. Whether it's worth doing depends on how soon the migration retires depth labels. **Revisit condition:** at E9 or once the migration plan is sketched; if migration is near, defer; if migration is far, execute as holding-pattern fix.

3. **Candidate 4 (fault-injection testing of Pattern A1 fallback paths) — independent of migration.** Tests fallback robustness; not tied to depth architecture. Eligible regardless of where the migration question lands. **Revisit condition:** E9 or any future session where evidence of fallback-path robustness becomes material.

4. **Candidate 5 (HTTP-layer verification) — independent of migration.** Tests the route's HTTP surface (auth, rate-limit, JSON parsing, R20a distress-check); not tied to depth architecture. Eligible regardless. **Revisit condition:** E9 or as part of pre-launch readiness work.

5. **Candidate 6 (move to a non-rollout Priority sequence item) — independent of migration.** Capability matrix work; ethical safeguards (R17, R19, R20); Agent Trust Layer (R18). None depend on depth architecture. **Revisit condition:** founder's strategic call at E9 open.

6. **Whether the architectural framing is the founder's settled view or a working hypothesis.** Recorded here as the founder's stated design intent at E8. Whether it survives further reflection, evidence, or stress-testing is a question for E9+. **Revisit condition:** any future session where evidence (cost data, output-quality samples, migration sketch) materially informs the framing.

## 2026-05-04 — D-E9-ADR003-AC8-AND-CACHE-DRIFT-RESOLVED-2026-05-04

**Decision:** Sub-session E9. Three-part decision: (1) **ADR-003 — Depth-as-Migration-Scaffolding for the Translation-Sandwich Architecture** drafted and adopted in the same session. Promotes the framing captured at D-E8-CLEANUP-AND-DEPTH-FRAMING-2026-05-04 §(3) from session insight to architectural decision. Names the target architecture (text → Layer 1 LLM extracting schema → Layer 2 deterministic mechanism application in code → Layer 3 LLM producing per-consumer prose), names today's reality (depth-tier does six bundled jobs across 25+ consumers), names the migration sequence (M1 pilot on `/api/reason` → M2 score family → M3 mentor family → M4 skill family → M5 scaffolding retirement) with risk classes per step, and names the seven retirement triggers required for M5. Founder approved verbatim with no edits. (2) **AC8 — Translation-Sandwich Architectural Constraint** added to `/manifest.md` under "Architectural Constraints" between AC7 and the Knowledge Gaps Register section. Wording per ADR-003 §"AC8 candidate wording", adopted verbatim. AC8 binds new pre-M5 consumers to the bundled-depth engine to prevent two engine surfaces running simultaneously during migration; AC8 also names ADR-003 as the source of the migration plan and retirement triggers. (3) **Cache drift resolved** under the cache's own update discipline. Four references in `/adopted/` updated from "AC1–AC7" to "AC1–AC8": `/adopted/standing-protocol-cache.md` (three references — line 46 §"Element 2 — Canonical-source read sequence", line 317 §"Cache update discipline", and line 330 §"Cross-references") and `/adopted/rag-mentor-alt3/migration-plan.md` (one reference — line 22). Historical handoffs and the predecessor next-session prompt are intentionally not edited (snapshot semantics; they accurately record manifest state at session time).

**Reasoning:** Sub-session E9 opened with a candidate menu of five paths reframed through the depth-architecture migration lens. Founder picked Candidate H (depth-architecture migration, the headline) and within H picked H1 (codify to ADR-003) over H2 (stress-test first). The choice signals that the framing is settled enough to codify; cost data and stress-test data follow at the pilot session (M1). AC8 was surfaced as a separate question per the project instructions's protection of governing documents; founder picked "Adopt verbatim this session" over deferral, edited wording, or rejection — signalling that the binding force on new pre-M5 consumers is wanted as a guardrail against parallel engine surfaces during migration. Cache drift resolution is the standing discipline triggered by any manifest amendment; three references found via grep, only `/adopted/` references updated.

**Reasoning:** Resolves ADR-004 §4.2's deferral of per-mechanism deterministic rules. Founder selected "all recommended" across six load-bearing decisions surfaced at session open: (1) ADR depth = pseudocode + citations + lookup tables in full; (2) Layer2Assessment output completeness = always emit every field with empty/null defaults (parallels ADR-005's L2a); (3) control filter classification when agent says "unspecified" = lookup table + flag in `disambiguation_required`; (4) passion → false-judgement mapping = hybrid (4 root defaults + 20 sub-species refinements); (5) Cicero's five questions deterministic application = per-circle honourability/advantageousness grades (1–3) + decision-tree resolution + inner-circle tie-break; (6) iterative refinement single-snapshot = sparse output with `direction_of_travel: "single_snapshot"`. ### Amendment to D-M1-CP3 — Post-harness in-session fix (2026-05-04)

**Trigger:** Founder ran the real-Sonnet harness from this session's close (`npx tsx scripts/verify-translation-sandwich.ts`); 73/77 checks passed; 4 failures all of the same root cause.

**Failure pattern:** Phase 5's per-fixture hard-assertion `direction_of_travel=single_snapshot → prose contains single-snapshot phrasing` failed for F1, F3, F4 (all three fixtures with `direction_of_travel === 'single_snapshot'`); F2 passed because its `direction_of_travel === 'stable'`. The cross-fixture marginal-case coverage assertion also failed (no fixture surfaced the marginal phrasing). Greek-identifier consistency was clean across all four fixtures — the LLM honoured the JSON contract and did not invent assessment content. The LLM-generated philosophical_reflection + improvement_guidance + summary fields were all rich, accurate Stoic prose; the LLM silently skipped the single-snapshot constraint sentence despite the prompt's explicit instruction.

The fallback prose ALSO omitted the single-snapshot phrasing because the original §6 fallback design at `fallbackPhilosophicalReflection` had no `direction_of_travel` handling. Both the LLM and the fallback paths failed the marginal-case discipline for `single_snapshot` — the discipline was instructed but not exemplified.

**AI diagnosis:** the original ADR-007 §3 OUTPUT example used the F1-style phone-checking case but did NOT include the single-snapshot sentence in the example's philosophical_reflection. Per the PR5 lesson from M1-CP1 + M1-CP2, the LLM honours worked OUTPUT examples more reliably than written instruction. The marginal-case discipline was named in the instruction but not demonstrated in the example, so the LLM treated it as optional. The fallback was not designed against the same contract.

**Founder direction:** "Fix in this session — Recommended."

**Fix applied (in-session amendment):**

1. ADR-007 §3 PROSE FIELDS instruction: philosophical_reflection word budget expanded from 2–3 sentences (~40–80 words) to 2–4 sentences (~40–110 words) to accommodate the marginal-case sentence; explicit MANDATORY clause added to the philosophical_reflection bullet specifying that single-snapshot phrasing is required when applicable; the marginal-case bullet for direction_of_travel now points the LLM to the OUTPUT example for the worked wording.
2. ADR-007 §3 OUTPUT example: philosophical_reflection's closing sentence appended with "This is a single snapshot; no trajectory data is available to assess your direction of travel." — the worked example now demonstrates the discipline.
3. ADR-007 §6 fallback documentation: the fallback's single-snapshot append behaviour documented; the summary's `is_kathekon === null` handling also documented.
4. ADR-007 Changelog: in-session amendment entry appended naming the harness result, the diagnosis, and the fix.
5. `/website/src/lib/translation-sandwich/layer3-prose.ts` `LAYER3_SYSTEM_PROMPT_API_REASON` constant: mirrored ADR-007 §3 amendment verbatim.
6. `/website/src/lib/translation-sandwich/layer3-prose.ts` `fallbackPhilosophicalReflection` function: appended single-snapshot sentence when `assessment.iterative_refinement.direction_of_travel === 'single_snapshot'`.

**In-sandbox verification of fix:** Smoke-test `smoke-layer3.ts` extended with 2 new assertions (F1 + MARG fixtures must contain single-snapshot phrasing in fallback's philosophical_reflection). Previously 32/32 passed; now 34/34 pass with the new assertions. `npx tsc --noEmit -p .` clean. The fallback path is verified in-session; the LLM path will be verified by the founder's between-sessions re-run.

**PR5 carry-forward update:**

- The original PR5 candidate ("LLM JSON-key fidelity requires concrete OUTPUT examples") **stays in watch (second-recurrence)**. The harness confirmed JSON keys were correct; the prophylactic application worked for what it was designed to catch.
- A **new PR5 candidate (first observation)** is logged this session: "**LLM marginal-case discipline (instruction to explicitly name undecidable verdicts) requires worked examples in the OUTPUT, not just bullet-point instruction.**" Trigger: harness Phase 5 surfaced the LLM omitting the single-snapshot sentence on 3 of 4 fixtures despite the prompt's explicit instruction. Resolution sketch: when ADR specifies marginal-case discipline, the OUTPUT example must demonstrate the discipline in concrete worked prose, not just name the rule in a bullet. This candidate stays in **candidate (first-observation)** status; will engage at M1-CP4 if observed again. Will be logged to `/operations/knowledge-gaps.md` at session close per PR5.

**M1-CP4 implications:**

- The next-session prompt for M1-CP4 already requires the founder's between-sessions re-run; that re-run is now verifying the AMENDED prompt + fallback, not the original.
- Open question 2 from this entry ("Marginal-case coverage adequacy of F1–F4") is **partially closed** by this amendment: the discipline now fires reliably in fallback (verified) and is more likely to fire reliably in LLM prose (to-be-verified by founder's re-run). The question of whether the assertion should be soft-warn vs hard-fail when ALL fixtures hit the marginal field remains open and is revisited at M1-CP4.

**Files touched (in addition to those in the parent entry):**

- `/adopted/adr/2026-05-04-layer3-prose-template-api-reason.md` — modified (in-session amendment to §3 + §6 + Changelog; ~365 → ~370 lines)
- `/website/src/lib/translation-sandwich/layer3-prose.ts` — modified (LAYER3_SYSTEM_PROMPT_API_REASON constant updated to mirror §3; fallbackPhilosophicalReflection extended with single-snapshot append; ~470 → ~485 lines)
- `/operations/decision-log.md` — modified (this amendment block appended; 2178 → ~2235 lines)

**Risk classification of the amendment:** Standard under 0d-ii (documentation amendment + non-safety module change). The amendment touches the prompt template + fallback function but does NOT change the module surface (exports unchanged), the validator (unchanged), the type definitions (unchanged), or the harness (unchanged). The route at `/api/reason` remains untouched (no import of `layer3-prose.ts` until M1-CP4 per ADR-004 §10.1). Critical Change Protocol NOT engaged.

To revert ONLY the amendment (preserving the M1-CP3 original work), revert the specific edits to ADR-007 §3 + §6 + Changelog and to `layer3-prose.ts` LAYER3_SYSTEM_PROMPT_API_REASON + fallbackPhilosophicalReflection. The smoke-test additions to `smoke-layer3.ts` are scratch artefacts in `/outputs/` and not committed.

```

```

If the LLM still omits the single-snapshot sentence after the amendment (re-run shows the same failure pattern), this is the **second observation** of the new PR5 candidate and the AI surfaces options at the next session: (a) tighten the prompt further (e.g., add a second worked OUTPUT example specifically for the marginal-case path); (b) demote the per-fixture hard-assert to soft-warn given ALL fixtures hit the marginal field; (c) accept the limitation and document it as a known M1-CP4 work item.

### Post-amendment harness re-run record — D-M1-CP3 (2026-05-04)

**Trigger:** Founder ran the harness after the first amendment landed.

**Result:** **78 / 79 checks passed** (improved from 73/77 pre-amendment). The amendment achieved its named purpose: the 3 previously-failing per-fixture single-snapshot assertions (F1.P5, F3.P5, F4.P5) all PASSED + the cross-fixture marginal-case coverage assertion PASSED. The 4 failures the founder approved fix-in-session for are all resolved.

**One residual failure (new pattern surfaced):** F1.P5 hard-asserted `is_kathekon: null → prose contains "cannot be determined"` and the LLM did not satisfy it. This is the **same structural pattern** as the single-snapshot drift — marginal-case discipline named in instruction but not demonstrated in OUTPUT example — now manifesting on a different marginal field (kathekon-null). The Greek-identifier consistency check was clean across all 4 fixtures; the LLM produced rich, substantively accurate prose; it silently skipped the kathekon-null discipline sentence.

**Fixture-driven visibility note:** F1's Layer 1 output is non-deterministic at temperature 0.2, so different runs produce different kathekon profiles. The previous run had F1 with `kathekon: contrary` (`is_kathekon === false`), so the kathekon-null assertion didn't fire. This run had F1 with `kathekon: marginal` (`is_kathekon === null`), and the assertion fired and failed. The latent issue was masked, not absent.

**PR5 disposition update:** The new candidate ("LLM marginal-case discipline requires worked OUTPUT examples") was logged at first observation (single-snapshot, last run). This run is the **second observation** (kathekon-null, this run). Per PR5: "Candidate (2nd recurrence) — observed twice. Promoted to 'watch' status with a proposed resolution sketch." Updated `/operations/knowledge-gaps.md` to reflect Watch (2nd recurrence) status with three resolution-sketch options. Third observation triggers permanent KG entry per PR5.

**Founder decision pending:** the founder is choosing whether to (a) apply a second in-session amendment fixing the kathekon-null pattern (and improvement_path_structured-null pre-emptively), (b) demote the marginal-case per-fixture hard-asserts to soft-warns, or (c) accept the residual failure and defer the comprehensive fix to M1-CP4. The chosen path becomes a second amendment block to this entry (or a new entry if a new ADR is created).

## 2026-05-04 — D-M1-CP4-PARALLEL-RUN-WIRING-2026-05-04

**Decision:** `/api/reason` reaches **Wired (parallel-run)** under the M1-CP4 deliverable per ADR-004 §10. The route is extended with an internal call path that runs Layer 1 → Layer 2 → Layer 3 strictly AFTER `runSageReason` returns and BEFORE the response is constructed. The user receives the bundled-depth result unchanged. The translation-sandwich result (or its failure category) is logged to a new Supabase `translation_sandwich_comparisons` table for offline comparison at M1-CP5. A new `translation_sandwich_cost_tracker` singleton-row table enforces the parallel-run cost cap defaults per ADR-004 §6.2 ($50 cumulative cost OR 1000 requests OR 14 days, whichever first). A new env variable `TRANSLATION_SANDWICH_PARALLEL_RUN` (read once at module load in `parallel-run.ts`) gates activation — when unset/"0", the parallel path is dormant and user-facing latency is identical to today. The verification harness `verify-translation-sandwich.ts` reaches **Wired (Phases 1–9)** with all 124 in-session checks passing (replay-cache mode against the four cached fixtures). All five load-bearing decisions surfaced before drafting (comparison-table schema; env-flag gating; cost-cap mechanics; failure-isolation logging; R20a perimeter preservation strategy with sequential-after-runSageReason composition + 500ms deadline) approved verbatim by founder ("approve all"). All eight named failure modes named in the Critical Change Protocol activation approved verbatim by founder ("approve all"). Production state at session close: deploys at next push, but with `TRANSLATION_SANDWICH_PARALLEL_RUN` unset → parallel path dormant; user-facing behaviour identical to today.

**Reasoning:** Per ADR-004 §10 M1-CP4 deliverable. M1-CP1 + CP2 + CP3 produced three Verified-standalone modules (`layer1-extractor.ts`, `layer2-mechanisms.ts`, `layer3-prose.ts`); M1-CP4 is the first session in the M1 arc that touches the user-facing route. The Critical Change Protocol applies (project instructions §0c-ii) because the route is one of the eight bound R20a perimeter routes (AC5) and the wiring is a deployment-config change (env flag). Failure isolation (ADR-004 §6.3) is the central design property: the user's response is the bundled-depth output, exactly as today; the parallel path's failures are logged but never propagate. The 500ms deadline grace AFTER `runSageReason` returns balances comparison-data completeness (most calls complete in <500ms) against user-facing latency (worst case +500ms). Cross-reference predecessor `D-M1-CP3-LAYER3-MODULE-AND-ADR007-2026-05-04` (Layer 3 module Verified standalone + ADR-007 Adopted + two in-session amendments resolving marginal-case discipline drift); `D-M1-CP2-LAYER2-MODULE-AND-ADR006-2026-05-04` (Layer 2 module + ADR-006); `D-M1-CP1-LAYER1-MODULE-AND-ADR005-2026-05-04` (Layer 1 module + ADR-005); `D-E10-ADR004-DRAFTED-AND-ADOPTED-2026-05-04` (E10 — ADR-004 codification, including the §6 + §7 deferrals this entry resolves).

**Files touched:**
- `/website/migrations/2026-05-04-translation-sandwich-comparisons.sql` — new (~70 lines; idempotent CREATE TABLE for the comparison log per ADR-004 §6.1; PII discipline (input_text_hash only, never input_text); JSONB columns for both engines' outputs per KG7; index on created_at; comments naming the 90-day retention policy + rollback path; copy/paste instructions for SQL Editor)
- `/website/migrations/2026-05-04-translation-sandwich-cost-tracker.sql` — new (~70 lines; idempotent CREATE TABLE for the singleton-row cost tracker per ADR-004 §6.2; CHECK CONSTRAINT id=1 enforces singleton; INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING seeds the row; comments naming the cap thresholds + reset SQL + rollback path; copy/paste instructions for SQL Editor)
- `/website/src/lib/translation-sandwich/parallel-run.ts` — new (~470 lines; exports `runParallelSandwich(params): Promise<void>` (NEVER throws; gated on env flag at module load); `runSandwichForHarness` (harness-facing; same orchestration without DB I/O); `isParallelRunEnabled` (test-only); `PARALLEL_RUN_CONFIG` (test-only constants object); `sonnetCostMicrocents` (cost utility; activated at M1-CP5 when extractFeatures + generateProse expose token usage). Internal: `getAdminClient()` lazy-creates the Supabase service-role client mirroring the established `r20a-cost-tracker.ts` pattern; `runSandwichInner` composes Layer 1 → Layer 2 → Layer 3 with deterministic Layer 3 fallback per ADR-007 §6 + ADR-004 §9.3; `runWithDeadline` wraps the sandwich with a 500ms grace via `Promise.race` against a setTimeout sentinel (per Step 1(e)); `readCostTracker` + `incrementCostTracker` defensively wrap Supabase reads/writes with try/catch (cap reads fail open per Step 1(c)); `logComparison` writes one row per request (try/catch; never throws). All Supabase writes are awaited per KG1 rule 2; no fire-and-forget; no module-level state)

**Open questions:**
1. **First-traffic comparison data behaviour at M1-CP5.** The parallel-run period accumulates comparison rows for offline analysis. The data may show schema-fidelity divergence, prose-quality differences, or cost patterns that require revisiting earlier ADRs. **Revisit at M1-CP5.**
2. **Per-layer cost capture deferred.** `extractFeatures` + `generateProse` do not currently expose token usage to their callers, so per-layer cost is logged as null in the comparison table. The cost utility `sonnetCostMicrocents` is exported in `parallel-run.ts` for M1-CP5 wiring. **Revisit at M1-CP5.**
3. **Cost-tracker concurrency.** The increment uses read-modify-write rather than an atomic Postgres function. At single-user traffic levels this is fine; at higher concurrency rate it could under-count. **Revisit at M1-CP5** if measured request count diverges from independent counts.
4. **Cap-read fail-open posture.** When the cost-tracker read fails (transient Supabase blip), the parallel path proceeds for that one request rather than failing closed. Trade-off: keeps the parallel-run period running through transient blips, at the price of potentially over-running the cap by a small amount. **Revisit at M1-CP5** if observed cost exceeds expected by more than ~5%.
5. **Cutover criteria.** **Revisit at M1-CP5** based on the comparison data.
6. **Public deprecation timing.** ADR-004 §10.2 + the M1-CP6 ADR will name the timing. **Revisit at M1-CP5.**

## 2026-05-05 — D-M1-CP5-FIRST-PASS-DEFERRED-2026-05-05

**Decision:** M1-CP5 first-pass deferred. The cutover / revise / rollback decision is **not** taken at this session. The parallel run continues unchanged; M1-CP5 resumes in a future session once a meaningful sample of completed comparison rows has accumulated. No code change, no production change, no environment change at this session. The route remains **Wired (parallel-run)**; no status taxonomy advances.

**Reasoning:** The first data pull at +24h after the M1-CP4 wiring shows 37 total rows in `translation_sandwich_comparisons`, of which 25 are pre-fix `deadline_exceeded` artefacts (count matches the M1-CP4 close note exactly — 25 sends from `/admin/test-reason` before the deadline-removal refactor) and only 12 are `sandwich_completed`. 12 usable rows is below the prompt's "≥ 50–100 rows for any meaningful analysis" threshold (M1-CP4 next-session prompt §"Pre-conditions" §4). Cost data is null because per-layer token-usage capture is deferred (M1-CP4 Open Question 2: `extractFeatures` + `generateProse` do not expose token usage to their callers; `cumulative_cost_usd_microcents = 0` against `request_count = 37`). Latency data is informative — sandwich p50 ≈ 25 s (Layer 1 ~13 s + Layer 3 ~12 s sequential) vs bundled-depth p50 ≈ 57 s, so cutover would meaningfully reduce user-facing latency — but a clear latency win is insufficient on its own to justify cutover commitment without cost evidence and without enough data to evaluate the rubric agreement rates (proximity match, virtue domains overlap, passions detected match, causal stage agreement, per-mechanism stage scores). Defer is the principled posture: the parallel run is dormant cost-wise (no real cost data yet) but is producing real comparison data slowly; nothing is at risk in waiting. Cross-reference predecessor `D-M1-CP4-PARALLEL-RUN-WIRING-2026-05-04`.

**Files touched:**
- `/operations/decision-log.md` — modified (this entry appended)
- `/operations/handoffs/founder/2026-05-05-sub-session-M1-CP5-first-pass-deferred-close.md` — new (this session's close, lean form per cache)
- `/operations/handoffs/founder/2026-05-05-M1-CP5-RESUME-NEXT-SESSION-PROMPT.md` — new (the resume prompt, lean form per cache; updated pre-conditions + same Part B procedure)

**Risk classification:** **Standard** under 0d-ii. Documentation-only; no code change; no production touch; no environment-variable change; no perimeter touched. AC4 / AC5 / AC7 / AC8 NOT engaged. PR1 / PR3 / PR4 / PR6 NOT engaged. Critical Change Protocol NOT engaged (per cache: governance category, lean form, Standard default).

**Rollback path:** None required (no code or production change made this session). If this entry is later judged incorrect, the established discipline is to append a corrective entry; do not edit this entry in place.

**Verification step (founder-performable):** Re-run the three SQL queries from this session at any time to monitor accumulation:
```sql
SELECT count(*) FILTER (WHERE translation_sandwich_output IS NOT NULL) AS sandwich_completed,
       count(*) AS total
FROM translation_sandwich_comparisons;
```
Expected at session close: `sandwich_completed = 12, total = 37`. M1-CP5 resume condition (founder's call): `sandwich_completed ≥ 50` OR `cap_reached = true` in `translation_sandwich_cost_tracker` OR 14 days have elapsed from `period_start` OR the founder chooses regardless.

**Open questions (carried into M1-CP5 resume):**
1. **Per-layer cost capture (M1-CP4 Open Q2 — promoted to standing follow-up).** `extractFeatures` + `generateProse` do not expose token usage to their callers; `parallel-run.ts` exports a `sonnetCostMicrocents` utility that is not yet called. Without this, the cost tracker stays at 0 indefinitely and the cap cannot trigger. Wiring it is a Standard-tier code session (one or both layer modules expose token usage; orchestrator multiplies and writes via the existing `incrementCostTracker`). Could be done as a small dedicated session before M1-CP5 resume, or folded into M1-CP5 resume itself. **Founder's call at next session-open.**
2. **Pre-fix `deadline_exceeded` rows.** 25 rows are deadline-artefact data, not engine-comparison data. Options: leave in place and filter at query time with `WHERE translation_sandwich_output IS NOT NULL` (cleanest, no DDL); OR delete via `DELETE FROM translation_sandwich_comparisons WHERE translation_sandwich_error = 'deadline_exceeded' AND created_at < '<post-fix timestamp>'` (founder runs in SQL Editor). **Revisit at M1-CP5 resume.**

## 2026-05-05 — D-M1-AC13-AC14-WIRING-REQUIRED-BEFORE-CUTOVER-2026-05-05

**Decision:** AC-13 (three-tier intake clarification: Tier 1 force / Tier 2 soft / Tier 3 deterministic-withhold) and AC-14 (withholding as deterministic kathekon — OPEN_DEFERRAL) **must be wired into M1's Layer 1 / Layer 2 / Layer 3 modules + the `/api/reason` route + the parallel-run orchestrator BEFORE M1-CP6 cutover**, not deferred to M2/M3/M4 consumers. The principled-withholding pedagogy is part of what the cutover commits to and cannot be missing from the user-facing engine when bundled-depth is retired. The M1 arc is therefore expanded with a new sub-session block (working labels: M1-CP4b → M1-CP4c → M1-CP4d → M1-CP4e → M1-CP4f) inserted between current M1-CP4 (parallel-run wiring done) and M1-CP5 (cutover decision). M1-CP5 first-pass remains deferred (per `D-M1-CP5-FIRST-PASS-DEFERRED-2026-05-05`); the resume conditions are revised to add AC-13 + AC-14 wiring as a new pre-condition in addition to the data-accumulation threshold.

**Reasoning:** The M1-CP5 first-pass close discussion surfaced that AC-13 + AC-14 are architecturally adopted in `/adopted/rag-mentor-alt3/` (the principle named in `ADR-RAG-MENTOR-ALT3-01.md`: withholding is kathekon, not a fallback; supported by `three-tier-intake.md`, `long-deferred-questions.md`, `reflect-endpoint-14b-deferral-resolution.md`, `residual-seams.md`, `d-a16-catalogue.md`) but **operationally absent in the M1 pilot for `/api/reason`**. Targeted grep across the four M1 ADRs (ADR-004 / ADR-005 / ADR-006 / ADR-007) and `/website/src/lib/translation-sandwich/` returned zero matches for the AC-13/AC-14 vocabulary (`OPEN_DEFERRAL`, `EUPATHEIA_BOUNDARY`, `PRAXIS_MOTIVATION_AMBIGUITY`, `ELEMENT_FUSION`, `SCOPE_AMBIGUITY`, `TEMPORAL_AMBIGUITY`, `STATED_OPERATIVE_CONFLICT`, `STATED_EQUANIMITY_UNVERIFIED`, three-tier, Tier 1/2/3, sit with, AC-13, AC-14). Cutover at M1-CP6 without these mechanisms would commit a translation-sandwich engine that does not honour the architecturally adopted withholding-as-kathekon discipline. R0 oikeiosis intent (the engine reasons by principled mechanism, not by LLM defaults) explicitly named in ADR-004 §"Consequences" cannot be claimed at cutover if the principled-withholding mechanism is absent. Cross-reference: `D-M1-CP5-FIRST-PASS-DEFERRED-2026-05-05` (the deferral entry that surfaced this gap); `ADR-RAG-MENTOR-ALT3-01-translation-sandwich-deterministic-engine.md` AC-12 + AC-13 + AC-14; `/adopted/rag-mentor-alt3/three-tier-intake.md` (Tier 1/2/3 specification); `/adopted/rag-mentor-alt3/long-deferred-questions.md` (the practitioner-sits-with-question principle + 7-day floor); `/adopted/rag-mentor-alt3/reflect-endpoint-14b-deferral-resolution.md` (the "sit with this" UI surface); `/adopted/rag-mentor-alt3/d-a16-catalogue.md` (deferred-question stems).
